> I'm working on a small task management app and want to use you as my pair     
  throughout. CLAUDE.md in the repo root has my conventions, so please read it  
  first.                                                                        
                                                                                
  The goal is a small task management app. .NET 9 Web API with EF Core code     
  first                                                                         
  on my local SQL Server Express (localhost\SQLEXPRESS), and a simple React +   
  Vite                                                                          
  front end on top. Users can list tasks (filter by status and priority), view  
  one,                                                                          
  create, update, and soft delete. There also needs to be a                     
  GET /api/tasks/summary endpoint that uses raw SQL, not EF, to return task     
  counts                                                                        
  grouped by status and priority.                                               
                                                                                
  I don't want any code yet. I'd like to prepare a plan first. Can you propose: 
                                                                                
  - how you'd lay out the solution (api, tests, ui), and whether a small shared 
    library is worth it here                                                    
  - how you'd model the Task entity and handle soft delete                      
  - how you'd do validation and a global exception handler so every error comes 
    back in the same shape                                                      
  - where logging should go                                                     
  - how we'd seed 10 or so sample tasks                                         
  - how we'd test it. so I want failure cases                                   
    and filtering covered too                                                   
  - a breakdown into small steps where each one can be built, tested and        
  committed                                                                     
    on its own                                                                  
                                                                                
  For Security taking CORS, config/secrets and                                  
  the raw SQL that we should handle properly from the start.                    
                                                                                
  If you're unsure about something , tell me instead of picking                 
  quietly. I'd rather decide than find out later. Don't start implementing      
  until we confirm the plan.

     ╭─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
     │ Task Manager — Implementation Plan                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Context                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The repo is empty except for CLAUDE.md, .gitignore and                  │
     │ docs/claude-logs. We're building a small task manager:                  │
     │ a .NET 9 Web API (EF Core Code First, SQL Server Express                │
     │ localhost\SQLEXPRESS, DB TaskManagerDb)                                 │
     │ with a React + Vite UI. Features: list (filter by status/priority), get │
     │ by id, create, update, soft delete,                                     │
     │ and GET /api/tasks/summary using raw SQL (not EF) for counts grouped by │
     │ status and priority.                                                    │
     │ Environment checked: .NET SDK 9.0.307 is installed (along with 6/7/8),  │
     │ Node 26 / npm 11.                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decisions made with you                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Test DB: SQLEXPRESS TaskManagerDb_Tests + Respawn · Raw SQL: plain      │
     │ ADO.NET · Summary: flat list ·                                          │
     │ Seeding: HasData in migration.                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Defaults I picked: say if you want any changed: int identity ids; PUT   │
     │ full update with                                                        │
     │ rowversion → 409 on conflict; DataAnnotations (no FluentValidation); no │
     │ pagination on the list (small                                           │
     │ dataset); no authentication; built-in logging (no Serilog); UI calls    │
     │ the API directly via                                                    │
     │ VITE_API_BASE_URL + CORS (no Vite proxy); FluentAssertions not used     │
     │ (licence change in v8), plain xUnit Assert.                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Solution layout                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ TaskManager.sln                                                         │
     │ global.json                      # pins SDK 9.0.307 (rollForward:       │
     │ latestPatch)                                                            │
     │ Directory.Build.props            # net9.0, Nullable, ImplicitUsings,    │
     │ TreatWarningsAsErrors, XML docs on                                      │
     │ Directory.Packages.props         # central package mgmt, every          │
     │ Microsoft.* pinned to 9.0.x                                             │
     │ src/TaskManager.Api/                                                    │
     │   Controllers/  TasksController.cs                                      │
     │   Contracts/    TaskResponse, CreateTaskRequest, UpdateTaskRequest,     │
     │ TaskQuery, TaskSummaryResponse                                          │
     │   Domain/       TaskItem.cs, TaskItemStatus.cs, TaskPriority.cs,        │
     │ NotFoundException.cs                                                    │
     │   Data/         TaskDbContext.cs,                                       │
     │ Configurations/TaskItemConfiguration.cs, SeedData.cs, Migrations/       │
     │   Services/     ITaskService/TaskService (EF),                          │
     │ ITaskSummaryService/TaskSummaryService (raw SQL)                        │
     │   Infrastructure/ GlobalExceptionHandler.cs,                            │
     │ ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs                                          │
     │   Program.cs                                                            │
     │ tests/TaskManager.Tests/                                                │
     │   Unit/         validation + mapping tests (no DB)                      │
     │   Integration/  WebApplicationFactory + real SQL Server test DB, one    │
     │ class per endpoint                                                      │
     │ src/task-manager-ui/                                                    │
     │   src/api/      httpClient.js (base URL, ProblemDetails parsing),       │
     │ tasksApi.js                                                             │
     │   src/components/ TaskList, TaskFilters, TaskDetail, TaskForm,          │
     │ TaskSummary, ErrorBanner (one per file)                                 │
     │   src/hooks/    useTasks.js etc.                                        │
     │ Shared library: not worth it. The only C# consumers are the API and its │
     │ tests; the UI is JS so it                                               │
     │ can't share DTOs. A separate Core/Infrastructure split adds projects    │
     │ and ceremony for ~5 endpoints.                                          │
     │ Folders inside the API project give the same separation; easy to        │
     │ extract later if the app grows.                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Entity is named TaskItem (not Task) to avoid clashing with              │
     │ System.Threading.Tasks.Task;                                            │
     │ the table is still Tasks and the route is still /api/tasks.             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 2. Task entity & soft delete                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ TaskItem: Id (int identity), Title (required, max 200), Description     │
     │ (nullable, max 2000),                                                   │
     │ Status (enum: Todo, InProgress, Done), Priority (enum: Low, Medium,     │
     │ High), DueDate (DateOnly?),                                             │
     │ CreatedAt / UpdatedAt (DateTimeOffset, UTC), IsDeleted (bool, default   │
     │ false), DeletedAt                                                       │
     │ (DateTimeOffset?), RowVersion (rowversion, optimistic concurrency).     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Enums stored as strings: HasConversion<string>().HasMaxLength(20);    │
     │   JSON uses JsonStringEnumConverter.                                    │
     │ - Global query filter HasQueryFilter(t => !t.IsDeleted).                │
     │ - Index on (IsDeleted, Status, Priority) to support filtering and the   │
     │   summary query.                                                        │
     │ - Soft delete done explicitly in TaskService.DeleteAsync (set           │
     │   IsDeleted, DeletedAt), not via an                                     │
     │   interceptor — simpler and obvious. Deleting a missing or              │
     │   already-deleted task returns 404.                                     │
     │ - Timestamps set in the service from an injected TimeProvider           │
     │   (deterministic in tests).                                             │
     │ - Update = PUT (full replace) carrying rowVersion; stale version → 409  │
     │   Conflict.                                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 3. Validation & one error shape                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Every error is RFC 7807 application/problem+json with traceId:          │
     │ - Validation: DataAnnotations on request DTOs + [ApiController]         │
     │   automatic 400 →                                                       │
     │   ValidationProblemDetails (errors: { field: [msgs] }). Invalid enum    │
     │   strings / malformed JSON /                                            │
     │   bad query values also land here via model binding. A small custom     │
     │   attribute covers rules like                                           │
     │   "enum value must be defined".                                         │
     │ - Exceptions: IExceptionHandler (GlobalExceptionHandler) +              │
     │   AddProblemDetails():                                                  │
     │   NotFoundException → 404, DbUpdateConcurrencyException → 409,          │
     │   BadHttpRequestException → 400,                                        │
     │   anything else → 500 with generic title/detail. Never includes         │
     │   exception message or stack trace.                                     │
     │ - UseStatusCodePages() so bare 404/405 (unknown routes) also return     │
     │   ProblemDetails.                                                       │
     │ - CustomizeProblemDetails adds traceId and instance consistently.       │
     │ - UI httpClient.js parses ProblemDetails once and surfaces title +      │
     │   field errors to forms.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 4. Logging (built-in ILogger, no Serilog)                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Middleware boundary: GlobalExceptionHandler logs unhandled exceptions │
     │   at Error (full exception,                                             │
     │   server-side only) with traceId; handled 4xx at Warning/Information.   │
     │   UseHttpLogging limited to                                             │
     │   method/path/status/duration (no bodies).                              │
     │ - Service boundary: structured messages, e.g. "Created task {TaskId}    │
     │   with priority {Priority}",                                            │
     │   "Task {TaskId} not found", "Soft-deleted task {TaskId}", summary      │
     │   query timing at Debug.                                                │
     │ - Never log request bodies or connection strings. Levels configured in  │
     │   appsettings.                                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Seeding ~10 sample tasks (decided: HasData in migration)             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - TaskItemConfiguration calls HasData(SeedData.Tasks) with 12 tasks:    │
     │   fixed Ids 1–12, fixed                                                 │
     │   literal CreatedAt/UpdatedAt/DueDate values (no DateTime.UtcNow,       │
     │   otherwise every new migration                                         │
     │   re-emits the seed rows), covering every status × priority             │
     │   combination, one or two pre-soft-deleted.                             │
     │ - Seed data lives in Data/SeedData.cs; it ships in the migration, so it │
     │   will exist in any DB the                                              │
     │   migration is applied to (you accepted this trade-off).                │
     │ - Schema + seed applied with dotnet ef database update (README          │
     │   documents it).                                                        │
     │ - Test DB: migration inserts the seed rows; Respawn wipes Tasks before  │
     │   each test so tests build their                                        │
     │   own fixture data and don't depend on seed contents. One test asserts  │
     │   the seed exists after migrating.                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. Raw SQL summary                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decided: plain ADO.NET (Microsoft.Data.SqlClient                        │
     │ SqlConnection/SqlCommand, ExecuteReaderAsync,                           │
     │ ordinal-based reads, enum strings parsed back with Enum.Parse).         │
     │ Connection string comes from                                            │
     │ IConfiguration / options, not from the DbContext. Constant SQL string,  │
     │ no concatenation:                                                       │
     │ SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) AS Count                              │
     │ FROM dbo.Tasks WHERE IsDeleted = 0                                      │
     │ GROUP BY Status, Priority;                                              │
     │ - Must filter IsDeleted = 0 manually (global query filter doesn't apply │
     │   to raw SQL) — test covers this.                                       │
     │ - Any future filters go through typed SqlParameters only.               │
     │ - Same ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection; async with                  │
     │   CancellationToken; ORDER BY Status, Priority                          │
     │   for stable output.                                                    │
     │ - Decided response: flat list [{ "status": "Todo", "priority": "High",  │
     │   "count": 3 }, ...], only                                              │
     │   combinations that have tasks; the UI computes any totals. Empty DB →  │
     │   [].                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 7. Security from the start                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - CORS: named policy; origins from config Cors:AllowedOrigins (dev:     │
     │   http://localhost:5173);                                               │
     │   explicit methods (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE) and Content-Type header; never │
     │   AllowAnyOrigin. Startup                                               │
     │   fails fast if the list is empty outside Development.                  │
     │ - Config/secrets: appsettings.json has no connection string; dev value  │
     │   (Windows auth, no secret)                                             │
     │   in appsettings.Development.json; other environments via env var       │
     │   ConnectionStrings__DefaultConnection or user-secrets. Startup         │
     │   validates the connection string exists.                               │
     │   README documents changing it for another machine. Connection string   │
     │   includes TrustServerCertificate=True                                  │
     │   for local Express only.                                               │
     │ - Raw SQL: parameterized only (above); EF handles everything else.      │
     │ - Input: max lengths, enum checks, route ids constrained                │
     │   {id:int:min(1)}, request body size default.                           │
     │ - HTTPS redirection + HSTS outside Development.                         │
     │ - No authentication — not requested; flagging that every endpoint is    │
     │   anonymous.                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 8. Testing                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ API (xUnit) — WebApplicationFactory<Program> against a separate         │
     │ TaskManagerDb_Tests DB on                                               │
     │ SQLEXPRESS (migrated once per run, data reset between tests). Real SQL  │
     │ Server is needed because the summary                                    │
     │ SQL and string-enum/rowversion behaviour must be verified for real; EF  │
     │ InMemory can't run raw SQL.                                             │
     │ Libraries: xUnit, Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Testing 9.0.x, Respawn for   │
     │ resets, plain Assert.                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Cases:                                                                  │
     │ - Create: valid → 201 + Location; missing/blank title → 400             │
     │   errors.Title; title >200 → 400;                                       │
     │   invalid status/priority string → 400; malformed JSON → 400            │
     │   ProblemDetails.                                                       │
     │ - Get: existing → 200; unknown id → 404 ProblemDetails; soft-deleted id │
     │   → 404; id=0 → 404/400.                                                │
     │ - List: no filter; by status; by priority; both; filter matching        │
     │   nothing → empty array;                                                │
     │   invalid filter value → 400; deleted tasks never returned.             │
     │ - Update: valid → 200 with new values + UpdatedAt; unknown → 404;       │
     │   deleted → 404; invalid body → 400;                                    │
     │   stale rowVersion → 409.                                               │
     │ - Delete: → 204, then GET 404, row still in DB with IsDeleted=1; delete │
     │   twice → 404.                                                          │
     │ - Summary: counts correct for a known fixture; combinations with no     │
     │   tasks absent; excludes deleted;                                       │
     │   empty DB → [].                                                        │
     │ - Errors: forced exception (test-only endpoint registered in the        │
     │   factory) → 500 ProblemDetails with                                    │
     │   no stack trace/message; unknown route → 404 ProblemDetails.           │
     │ - CORS: preflight from allowed origin gets Access-Control-Allow-Origin; │
     │   other origin doesn't.                                                 │
     │   Unit: DTO validation attributes, entity↔DTO mapping.                  │
     │   UI (Vitest + React Testing Library): api layer (URL/query building,   │
     │   ProblemDetails → error object),                                       │
     │   TaskFilters changes call API with right params, TaskForm shows server │
     │   validation errors, delete flow.                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 9. Step-by-step breakdown (each: build → test → you commit)             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Scaffold: sln, global.json, Directory.*.props, Api + Tests projects, │
     │    /health endpoint, smoke test.                                        │
     │ 2. Cross-cutting: ProblemDetails + GlobalExceptionHandler, status code  │
     │    pages, logging config,                                               │
     │    CORS from config, config validation. Tests: 500 shape, 404 shape,    │
     │    CORS.                                                                │
     │ 3. Data model: TaskItem, enums, DbContext, configuration, soft-delete   │
     │    filter, initial migration,                                           │
     │    test DB fixture. Test: filter hides deleted rows.                    │
     │ 4. Seeding: SeedData + HasData, new migration. Test: seed rows present  │
     │    after migrate.                                                       │
     │ 5. Create + Get by id: DTOs, validation, service, controller. Tests     │
     │    above.                                                               │
     │ 6. List with filters. Tests above.                                      │
     │ 7. Update (PUT + concurrency). Tests above.                             │
     │ 8. Soft delete. Tests above.                                            │
     │ 9. Summary (raw SQL). Tests above.                                      │
     │ 10. UI scaffold: Vite app, api layer, task list + filters, Vitest       │
     │     setup.                                                              │
     │ 11. UI detail + create/edit form with server error display.             │
     │ 12. UI delete + summary view; README final pass (README updated in      │
     │     every step anyway).                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verification                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - dotnet test green at every backend step (requires SQLEXPRESS          │
     │   running).                                                             │
     │ - dotnet ef database update then dotnet run → exercise endpoints via a  │
     │   checked-in TaskManager.Api.http.                                      │
     │ - npm test (Vitest) and npm run dev for UI steps; manual check          │
     │   list/filter/create/edit/delete/summary.                               │

  about

  descending, then CreatedDate descending) so we have a clear LINQ filter and

  me

     ~\.claude\plans\pasted-content-id-0656-i-m-working-expressive-umbrella.md ·

     Task Manager — Implementation Plan (rev 2)

     Context

     The repo is empty except for CLAUDE.md, .gitignore and docs/claude-logs.
     We're building a small task manager:
     a .NET 9 Web API (EF Core Code First, SQL Server Express
     localhost\SQLEXPRESS, DB TaskManagerDb)
     with a React + Vite UI. Features: list (filter by status/priority), get by
     id, create, update, soft delete,
     and GET /api/tasks/summary using raw SQL (not EF) for counts grouped by
     status and priority.
     Environment: .NET SDK 9.0.307 installed (plus 6/7/8), Node 26 / npm 11.

     Decisions

     - Test DB: SQLEXPRESS TaskManagerDb_Tests + Respawn (ignores
       __EFMigrationsHistory)
     - Raw SQL: plain ADO.NET (Microsoft.Data.SqlClient)
     - Summary: flat list, only combinations that have tasks
     - Seeding: HasData in migration, ~15 tasks
     - Priority: Low, Medium, High, Critical. Entity has AssignedTo. Timestamps
       are CreatedDate / ModifiedDate
     - global.json: 9.0.100 with rollForward: latestFeature (stays on 9.0.x and
       never moves to 10)
     - Steps 1 and 2 merged into one iteration
     - Defaults (change any you like): int identity ids; DataAnnotations; no
       list pagination; no auth;
       built-in logging; UI calls API via VITE_API_BASE_URL + CORS; plain xUnit
       Assert

     1. Solution layout

     TaskManager.sln, global.json, Directory.Build.props, 
     Directory.Packages.props (central, Microsoft.* 9.0.x)
     src/TaskManager.Api/   Controllers/ Contracts/ Domain/ Data/ Services/ 
     Infrastructure/ Program.cs
     tests/TaskManager.Tests/  Unit/  Integration/
     src/task-manager-ui/   src/api/ src/components/ src/hooks/
     No shared library: the API and its tests are the only C# consumers, and the
     UI is JS. Entity is TaskItem
     so it doesn't clash with System.Threading.Tasks.Task. The table is Tasks
     and the route is /api/tasks.

     2. Entity & soft delete

     TaskItem: Id int; Title required, max 200; Description max 2000 (nullable);
     Status (Todo,
     InProgress, Done); Priority (Low, Medium, High, Critical); AssignedTo
     (nullable, max 100);
     DueDate (DateOnly?); CreatedDate / ModifiedDate (DateTimeOffset, UTC, from
     injected
     TimeProvider); IsDeleted; DeletedDate (DateTimeOffset?); RowVersion
     (rowversion).
     - Enums stored as strings (HasConversion<string>(), max 20). The API uses
       JsonStringEnumConverter.
     - Global filter !IsDeleted. Index on (IsDeleted, Status, Priority).
     - Soft delete happens explicitly in the service. Deleting a missing or
       already-deleted task returns 404.

     Sorting caveat: because enums are stored as strings, a plain ORDER BY 
     Priority sorts alphabetically
     (Critical, High, Low, Medium). Both the LINQ and the SQL sort map priority
     to a rank with a CASE:
     - LINQ (list): query.Where(status?).Where(priority?)
       .OrderByDescending(t => t.Priority == Critical ? 4 : t.Priority == High ?
       3 : t.Priority == Medium ? 2 : 1)
       .ThenByDescending(t => t.CreatedDate). EF translates this to SQL CASE. A
       test checks the order.
     - Summary SQL:
     SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) AS [Count]
     FROM dbo.Tasks
     WHERE IsDeleted = 0
     GROUP BY Status, Priority
     ORDER BY CASE Status WHEN 'Todo' THEN 1 WHEN 'InProgress' THEN 2 WHEN
     'Done' THEN 3 END,
              CASE Priority WHEN 'Critical' THEN 1 WHEN 'High' THEN 2 WHEN
     'Medium' THEN 3 WHEN 'Low' THEN 4 END;
       The SQL is a constant string. Any future filters will go only through
     typed SqlParameters.

     3. Update: PUT vs PATCH (decide before step 7)

     My recommendation is to keep PUT for the edit form and also add PATCH 
     /api/tasks/{id}/status
     with body { "status": "Done" }. It returns the updated TaskResponse,
     including the new rowVersion.
     - A dropdown that has to send title, description, assignee etc. back just
       to change status is wasteful.
       It also risks overwriting another user's edits with stale values. That
       second problem is worse than a 409.
     - A single-field, purpose-built endpoint is easy to validate with
       DataAnnotations. I'm not using JSON Patch
       (RFC 6902): it needs Newtonsoft and generic operations are hard to
       validate.
     - The status PATCH doesn't require a rowVersion: last write wins for that
       one field, which is the sensible
       behaviour for a status toggle. PUT keeps rowVersion → 409, because it
       replaces every field.
     - The UI must replace its cached task (rowVersion included) with the PATCH
       response so a later PUT doesn't 409.
       If you prefer one endpoint only, the alternative is keeping PUT and
       having the dropdown send the full task.

     4. Validation & errors (one shape: RFC 7807 ProblemDetails + traceId)

     - [ApiController] + DataAnnotations → 400 ValidationProblemDetails. Bad
       enum, JSON or query values also end up here.
     - GlobalExceptionHandler : IExceptionHandler + AddProblemDetails():
       NotFoundException→404,
       DbUpdateConcurrencyException→409, BadHttpRequestException→400, everything
       else→500 with a generic message.
       No stack trace and no exception message is sent to the client.
     - UseStatusCodePages() so unknown routes and 405s also return
       ProblemDetails. CustomizeProblemDetails adds traceId.

     5. Logging

     Built-in ILogger. Exceptions are logged in the handler with the traceId
     (Error for 5xx, Warning for 4xx).
     UseHttpLogging records method, path, status and duration only (no bodies).
     Services log structured events
     ({TaskId}, {Priority}). Connection strings and bodies are never logged.

     6. Seeding (HasData)

     Data/SeedData.cs: 15 tasks with fixed Ids 1–15 and literal dates, so new
     migrations don't keep
     re-emitting the seed rows. 12 are active and cover all 12 status × priority
     combinations. 3 are soft-deleted.
     AssignedTo varies (e.g. "alice", "bob", "chen", "dana", plus a few
     unassigned).
     Seed rows also land in the test DB. Respawn wipes Tasks before each test,
     so tests build their own data.

     7. Security

     - CORS: named policy, origins from Cors:AllowedOrigins (dev
       http://localhost:5173), explicit methods
       (GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE) and the Content-Type header. No
       AllowAnyOrigin. Startup fails fast outside
       Development if the list is empty.
     - Config: appsettings.json has no connection string. The dev value (Windows
       auth, no secret) goes in
       appsettings.Development.json. Other environments use env var
       ConnectionStrings__DefaultConnection or
       user-secrets. The connection string is validated at startup.
     - Raw SQL is parameterized only. Route ids use {id:int:min(1)}. HTTPS
       redirection and HSTS outside Development.
     - No authentication (it wasn't requested).

     8. Testing

     - Integration (WebApplicationFactory<Program>, TaskManagerDb_Tests, Respawn
       with
       TablesToIgnore = ["__EFMigrationsHistory"]):
       - create (201 / 400 on title, enum and malformed JSON)
       - get (200 / 404 / deleted → 404)
       - list (each filter, both filters, no match, invalid value → 400, deleted
         excluded, sort order
         with Critical first and newest first within a priority)
       - update (200 / 404 / 400 / stale rowVersion → 409)
       - status PATCH, if approved
       - delete (204, then 404; row still present; deleting twice → 404)
       - summary (counts, absent combos, deleted excluded, ordering, empty → [])
       - errors (500 shape without stack trace, 404 route shape)
       - CORS (preflight allowed and denied)
     - Unit:
       - DTO validation
       - TaskService.CreateAsync with FakeTimeProvider
         (Microsoft.Extensions.TimeProvider.Testing
         9.0.x) asserting CreatedDate == ModifiedDate == fake now. It uses the
         EF InMemory provider (9.0.x),
         since this test only checks service logic and not SQL. Real SQL
         behaviour stays in the integration tests.
     - UI (Vitest + RTL): the api layer (query building, ProblemDetails
       parsing), filters, form errors, delete flow.

     9. Steps (each: build → dotnet test → you commit)

     1. Scaffold + cross-cutting (merged): sln, global.json, Directory.*.props,
        Api + Tests projects,
        /health, ProblemDetails + GlobalExceptionHandler, status code pages,
        logging, CORS from config, config
        validation, README. Tests: health 200, 500 shape (test-only throwing
        endpoint), unknown route → 404
        ProblemDetails, CORS allowed and denied. No DB is needed yet: the
        connection string check comes in step 2.
     2. Data model + migration + soft-delete filter + test DB fixture (Respawn).
     3. Seeding (HasData, 15 tasks).
     4. Create + get by id (+ FakeTimeProvider unit test).
     5. List with filters + explicit sort.
     6. Update (PUT, + PATCH status if approved).
     7. Soft delete.
     8. Summary (ADO.NET).
     9. UI scaffold + api layer + list/filters.
     10. UI detail + create/edit form.
     11. UI delete + status dropdown + summary view; README pass.

     Verification for step 1

     dotnet build, dotnet test, then dotnet run --project src/TaskManager.Api
     and check:
     - GET /health → 200
     - GET /nope → 404 problem+json
     - a preflight OPTIONS with Origin: http://localhost:5173 returns the
       allow-origin header
     - the same preflight with Origin: http://evil.test does not

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Step 1 (scaffold plus cross-cutting concerns) is done and dotnet test passes
  all 25 tests. I haven't committed anything or started step 2. I also ran the
  real API once and checked the health endpoint, an unknown route and both
  preflights; all four behaved as expected.

  How to verify

  dotnet build
  dotnet test
  dotnet run --project src/TaskManager.Api --launch-profile http

  Then, in a second terminal (use curl.exe so PowerShell's curl alias doesn't
  get in the way):

  # Health check: expect 200 and "Healthy"
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/health

  # Unknown route: expect 404, Content-Type application/problem+json, with 
  instance and traceId
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/nope

  # Preflight from the UI origin: expect 204 with
  #   Access-Control-Allow-Origin: http://localhost:5173
  #   Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET,POST,PUT,PATCH,DELETE
  #   Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type
  curl.exe -i -X OPTIONS http://localhost:5157/health -H "Origin: 
  http://localhost:5173" -H "Access-Control-Request-Method: GET"

  # Preflight from an unknown origin: expect 204 with NO Access-Control-* 
  headers
  curl.exe -i -X OPTIONS http://localhost:5157/health -H "Origin: 
  http://evil.test" -H "Access-Control-Request-Method: GET"

  The same four requests are in src/TaskManager.Api/TaskManager.Api.http. To see
  the fail-fast check, run $env:ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT="Production"; dotnet run
  --project src/TaskManager.Api --no-launch-profile. Startup should stop
  because Cors:AllowedOrigins is empty.

  Things you should know

  - Disallowed request headers: ASP.NET Core still sends
    Access-Control-Allow-Origin when a preflight asks for a header we don't
    allow, such as Authorization. It just leaves that header out of
    Access-Control-Allow-Headers, and the browser blocks the request. My first
    test expected no origin header at all and failed, so I changed it to check
    this actual behaviour.
  - Middleware order: CORS runs before the exception handler so that 500
    responses also carry the CORS header. Without it the UI couldn't read the
    error body. There's a test for this.
  - Logging: I set the framework's ExceptionHandlerMiddleware log level to None.
    It would otherwise log every exception a second time, including 404s, at
    Error level. GlobalExceptionHandler does the logging with the traceId.
  - Test-only endpoint: TestErrorsController exists only in the test project.
    The test factory registers it, so it never ships in the API.
  - Version exception: Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk is 17.12.0. It's versioned
    separately from .NET and has no 9.0.x line. Everything else from Microsoft
    is pinned to 9.0.11.
  - Not in this step: the 409 mapping for EF's concurrency exception and the
    connection-string check both need EF, so they come in step 2.
  - Promp.txt: this file is in the repo root and untracked, but I didn't create
    it. Leave it out of the commit if it isn't meant to be there.

  Files to review

  TaskManager.sln, README.md

  - TaskManager.Api.csproj

  - Integration/ErrorHandlingTests.cs: 500 with no leaked message or stack
    trace, 404 from the exception, unknown route 404, 405, CORS header on errors
  - Integration/CorsTests.cs: allowed and unknown origins, allowed and
    disallowed headers, simple request
  - Integration/StartupValidationTests.cs: Production with no origins fails to
    start, wildcard origin fails to start
  - Unit/CorsSettingsValidatorTests.cs

     Step 2: Data model, DbContext, migration, test DB fixture

     Context

     Step 1 is committed (98efc4a: scaffold, ProblemDetails, CORS, health). Step
     2 adds persistence and has
     no endpoints yet: the TaskItem entity, enums, TaskDbContext with string
     enums and a soft-delete
     query filter, the InitialCreate migration, connection-string startup
     validation, and a SQL Server
     test-DB fixture with Respawn. Environment checked: dotnet-ef 9.0.10; the
     MSSQL$SQLEXPRESS service
     is running; sqlcmd is available.

     Packages (Directory.Packages.props, all pinned to 9.0.10 to match dotnet-ef
     exactly, so there's no tools-older-than-runtime warning)

     - API: Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.SqlServer 9.0.10,
       Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design 9.0.10 (PrivateAssets=all)
     - Tests: Respawn 6.2.1 (not a Microsoft package). Microsoft.Data.SqlClient
       comes transitively from the EF SqlServer provider.

     Entity & enums (src/TaskManager.Api/Domain/)

     - TaskItem: brief names only. Id (int identity), Title (string, required),
       Description (string?),
       Status, Priority, AssignedTo (string?), CreatedDate, ModifiedDate
       (DateTimeOffset, UTC),
       IsDeleted (bool). The only extra field is RowVersion (byte[], concurrency
       token).
       DueDate and DeletedDate from the earlier draft are dropped because they
       aren't in the brief.
     - TaskItemStatus { Todo, InProgress, Done }. It's not called TaskStatus,
       which would clash with
       System.Threading.Tasks.TaskStatus, just as Task clashes with TaskItem.
     - TaskPriority { Low, Medium, High, Critical }.

     DbContext & configuration (src/TaskManager.Api/Data/)

     - TaskDbContext with DbSet<TaskItem> Tasks. It applies configurations from
       the assembly.
     - Configurations/TaskItemConfiguration.cs
       (IEntityTypeConfiguration<TaskItem>):
       - table Tasks. Title nvarchar(200) required, Description nvarchar(2000),
         AssignedTo nvarchar(100)
       - Status/Priority: HasConversion<string>().HasMaxLength(20), plus check 
         constraints built
         from Enum.GetNames ([Status] IN ('Todo','InProgress','Done')). Rows
         written by raw SQL or by hand
         can't hold bad values, which the step-8 summary SQL relies on.
       - IsDeleted required (no DB default). RowVersion uses .IsRowVersion().
       - HasQueryFilter(t => !t.IsDeleted)
       - index IX_Tasks_IsDeleted_Status_Priority
     - DatabaseSettings options: bound from ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection,
       ValidateOnStart, with a
       clear error message if it's missing. AddTaskDatabase() extension in
       ServiceCollectionExtensions registers
       AddDbContext<TaskDbContext> using those options. EnableRetryOnFailure is
       off: it conflicts with user transactions and isn't needed locally.
     - appsettings.Development.json:
       Server=localhost\SQLEXPRESS;Database=TaskManagerDb;Trusted_Connection=Tru
       e;TrustServerCertificate=True
       (Windows auth, no secret). appsettings.json has no connection string.
     - /health stays a plain liveness check with no DB dependency, so step-1
       tests need no database.

     Migration

     dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --project src/TaskManager.Api 
     --output-dir Data/Migrations.
     It's applied manually with dotnet ef database update --project 
     src/TaskManager.Api; nothing migrates at app startup.
     EF 9 puts /// <inheritdoc /> on generated members. If CS1591 still fires
     under TreatWarningsAsErrors,
     I'll add an .editorconfig rule that silences CS1591 only for
     Data/Migrations/*.cs.

     Test DB fixture (tests/TaskManager.Tests/Integration/Database/)

     - TestDatabase: the connection string defaults to
       ...Database=TaskManagerDb_Tests... on SQLEXPRESS. Env var
       TASKMANAGER_TEST_CONNECTION can override it (for another machine or CI).
       Guard: it refuses to run unless the database name ends in _Tests, so
       Respawn can never wipe TaskManagerDb.
     - TaskManagerApiFactory adds ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection = the test
       connection string.
     - DatabaseFixture : IAsyncLifetime (xUnit collection fixture "Database").
       Once per run it does three things:
       - runs MigrateAsync(), which creates the DB if it's missing
       - builds a Respawner with DbAdapter.SqlServer and TablesToIgnore = 
         ["__EFMigrationsHistory"]
       - exposes ResetAsync() and CreateDbContext()
     - DB test classes join [Collection("Database")] and call ResetAsync() in
       InitializeAsync, so every test starts empty.
       Collections run serially against the shared DB. Non-DB tests keep running
       in parallel.

     Tests added in step 2

     - TaskDbContextTests (DB):
       - saving and reading back round-trips every field
       - Status and Priority are stored as strings (checked by reading the raw
         column)
       - the soft-delete filter hides deleted rows, and IgnoreQueryFilters()
         shows them
       - RowVersion is set on insert and changes on update
       - two contexts updating the same row → the second throws
         DbUpdateConcurrencyException
       - the check constraint rejects an invalid Status written with raw SQL
     - DatabaseFixtureTests: refuses a connection string whose DB name doesn't
       end in _Tests.
     - StartupValidationTests: a non-Development env with no connection string
       fails with the connection-string message.
       The existing Production/CORS test keeps working because startup
       validation collects every failure.
     - ErrorHandlingTests: TestErrorsController throws
       DbUpdateConcurrencyException → 409 ProblemDetails.

     409 for DbUpdateConcurrencyException: decided now

     - RowVersion arrives in this step, and so does the EF package that defines
       the exception. The token
       and its HTTP mapping belong together. Once they exist, any later
       SaveChanges that hits a concurrent edit
       would otherwise surface as a generic 500 (the PATCH status endpoint and
       delete included, not just PUT).
     - GlobalExceptionHandler is the single place where errors map to HTTP. The
       change is one switch arm
       (409, "Conflict", "The task was changed by someone else. Reload it and
       try again.") plus one test using
       the existing TestErrorsController pattern, the same way the 404 mapping
       was tested in step 1.
     - Step 6 then adds the real end-to-end test (a stale rowVersion on PUT
       → 409), with no handler change needed.
     - The case for waiting: until step 6 nothing can actually throw it, so it's
       a small amount of speculative code.
       I think that's outweighed by the 500 risk and by keeping step 6 focused
       on the endpoint.

     Files

     New:
     - Domain/TaskItem.cs, Domain/TaskItemStatus.cs, Domain/TaskPriority.cs
     - Data/TaskDbContext.cs, Data/Configurations/TaskItemConfiguration.cs,
       Data/Migrations/*
     - Infrastructure/DatabaseSettings.cs
     - test files Integration/Database/{TestDatabase,DatabaseFixture,DatabaseCol
       lection,TaskDbContextTests,DatabaseFixtureTests}.cs

     Edited:
     - Directory.Packages.props, TaskManager.Api.csproj,

      31 +## Data model                                                  
      32 +Table `dbo.Tasks` (entity `TaskItem`):                         
      33 +                                                               
      34 +| Column | Type | Notes |                                      
      35 +|--------|------|-------|                                      
      36 +| Id | int identity | PK |                                     
      37 +| Title | nvarchar(200) | required |                           
      38 +| Description | nvarchar(2000) | optional |                    
      39 +| Status | nvarchar(20) | `Todo`, `InProgress`, `Done`; enforce
         +d by `CK_Tasks_Status` |                                       
      40 +| Priority | nvarchar(20) | `Low`, `Medium`, `High`, `Critical`
         +; enforced by `CK_Tasks_Priority` |                            
      41 +| AssignedTo | nvarchar(100) | optional |                      
      42 +| CreatedDate / ModifiedDate | datetimeoffset | UTC |          
      43 +| IsDeleted | bit | soft delete; EF global query filter hides d
         +eleted rows |                                                  
      44 +| RowVersion | rowversion | optimistic concurrency (a conflicti
         +ng save returns 409) |                                         
      45 +                                                               
      46 +Index `IX_Tasks_IsDeleted_Status_Priority` supports filtering a
         +nd the summary query.                                          
      47 +Enums are stored as strings, so sorting by priority uses an exp
         +licit rank, never the column value.                            
      48 +                                                               
      49 +## Tests                                                       
      50 +Integration tests use a separate database, **`TaskManagerDb_Tes
         +ts`** on `localhost\SQLEXPRESS` (Windows auth).                
      51 +The test run creates and migrates it automatically, and Respawn
         + empties every table (except                                   
      52 +`__EFMigrationsHistory`) before each database test. As a safegu
         +ard, tests refuse to run against any database

● Step 2 is done: dotnet test passes all 38 tests (25 from step 1, 13 new). I
  haven't committed anything or started step 3, and I haven't touched your dev
  database. Creating TaskManagerDb is the first verification step below.

  Respawn: the build didn't complain about the ignore syntax, so nothing
  changed. It's written as TablesToIgnore = [new Table("__EFMigrationsHistory")]
  in DatabaseFixture.cs.

  How to verify

  dotnet build
  dotnet test                                                     # creates and 
  migrates TaskManagerDb_Tests itself
  dotnet ef database update --project src/TaskManager.Api         # creates 
  TaskManagerDb
  dotnet run --project src/TaskManager.Api --launch-profile http  # should start
  as before; GET /health returns 200

  Checking it in SSMS

  Connect to localhost\SQLEXPRESS with Windows auth, open a new query window on
  TaskManagerDb and run these. They also work on TaskManagerDb_Tests, which is
  where I ran them and got the expected results.

  USE TaskManagerDb;

  -- 1. Columns: Status/Priority nvarchar (MaxBytes 40 = 20 chars), RowVersion 
  shows as timestamp
  SELECT c.name AS ColumnName, t.name AS DataType, c.max_length AS MaxBytes,
         c.is_nullable AS Nullable, c.is_identity AS IsIdentity
  FROM sys.columns c JOIN sys.types t ON t.user_type_id = c.user_type_id
  WHERE c.object_id = OBJECT_ID(N'dbo.Tasks')
  ORDER BY c.column_id;

  -- 2. Check constraints: expect CK_Tasks_Status and CK_Tasks_Priority (SQL 
  Server shows IN (...) as OR's)
  SELECT name AS CheckConstraint, definition
  FROM sys.check_constraints
  WHERE parent_object_id = OBJECT_ID(N'dbo.Tasks');

  -- 3. Indexes: expect PK_Tasks (Id) and IX_Tasks_IsDeleted_Status_Priority
  SELECT i.name AS IndexName, i.type_desc,
         STRING_AGG(c.name, ', ') WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY ic.key_ordinal) AS
  KeyColumns
  FROM sys.indexes i
  JOIN sys.index_columns ic ON ic.object_id = i.object_id AND ic.index_id =
  i.index_id
  JOIN sys.columns c ON c.object_id = ic.object_id AND c.column_id =
  ic.column_id
  WHERE i.object_id = OBJECT_ID(N'dbo.Tasks')
  GROUP BY i.name, i.type_desc;

  -- 4. Migration history: one row, 20261120094209_InitialCreate, 9.0.10
  SELECT MigrationId, ProductVersion FROM dbo.__EFMigrationsHistory;

  -- 5. Constraint in action: this should FAIL with a CK_Tasks_Status conflict
  INSERT INTO dbo.Tasks (Title, Status, Priority, CreatedDate, ModifiedDate,
  IsDeleted)
  VALUES (N'bad', N'Blocked', N'Low', SYSUTCDATETIME(), SYSUTCDATETIME(), 0);
  In Object Explorer you can also open Databases → TaskManagerDb → Tables → 
  dbo.Tasks and look under Columns, Constraints and Indexes.

  Things you should know

  - Field names: TaskItem uses the brief's names exactly. RowVersion is the only
    extra field; DueDate and DeletedDate are gone. The status enum is called
    TaskItemStatus because TaskStatus would clash with a .NET type.
  - 409 mapping: a concurrency conflict now returns 409 "Conflict" with a fixed
    message. It never includes the exception text, and a test covers that.
  - Connection string check: the API now refuses to start when
    ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection is missing. /health still doesn't touch
    the database.
  - Test DB guard: tests refuse to run unless the database name ends in _Tests,
    so Respawn can't wipe TaskManagerDb by mistake. You can point them at
    another server with TASKMANAGER_TEST_CONNECTION.
  - Migration warnings: the generated migration files built without
    missing-XML-doc warnings, so I didn't need the .editorconfig exception.
  - Test file name: the plan called the guard tests DatabaseFixtureTests. I
    named the file TestDatabaseTests.cs because that's the class it tests.
  - Promp.txt: it's still untracked in the repo root.

     Step 3: Seed data via HasData

     Context

     Step 2 is committed (4b604af): TaskItem, TaskDbContext, the InitialCreate
     migration, and a test DB fixture
     with Respawn. Step 3 adds about 15 sample tasks through HasData, in their
     own migration. They give the
     UI and manual testing realistic data, and the soft-deleted rows let you
     check the query filter and the
     summary exclusion by eye. No endpoints change.

     Seed data: src/TaskManager.Api/Data/SeedData.cs

     public static class SeedData exposes IReadOnlyList<TaskItem> Tasks. The ids
     are fixed and every date is a
     literal new DateTimeOffset(2026, 1, d, h, 0, 0, TimeSpan.Zero) (UTC), never
     UtcNow, so future
     migrations don't re-emit the rows. ModifiedDate is always ≥ CreatedDate.
     CreatedDate values are all
     distinct, so the step-5 sort (priority desc, then CreatedDate desc) gives a
     deterministic order.

     ┌─────┬────────────┬──────────┬────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
     │ Id  │   Status   │ Priority │ AssignedTo │          Title           │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 1   │ Todo       │ Low      │ Bob        │ Archive old project      │
     │     │            │          │            │ files                    │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 2   │ Todo       │ Medium   │ Chen       │ Update team wiki         │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 3   │ Todo       │ High     │ Alice      │ Prepare sprint demo      │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 4   │ Todo       │ Critical │ Dana       │ Fix login outage         │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 5   │ InProgress │ Low      │ (null)     │ Tidy CSS variables       │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 6   │ InProgress │ Medium   │ Alice      │ Write API integration    │
     │     │            │          │            │ tests                    │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 7   │ InProgress │ High     │ Bob        │ Migrate CI to new        │
     │     │            │          │            │ runners                  │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
     │ 8   │ InProgress │ Critical │ Chen       │ Patch security           │
     │     │            │          │            │ vulnerability            │
     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤

     ├─────┼────────────┼──────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤

     │     │            │          │            │ (deleted)                │

     │ 15  │ Done       │ Low      │ (null)     │ Old test task (deleted)  │
     └─────┴────────────┴──────────┴────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

     - Rows 1–12 are active and cover each of the 12 status × priority
       combinations exactly once.
     - Rows 13–15 are soft-deleted and spread across statuses. That makes
       summary counts visibly differ if the
       IsDeleted = 0 filter is ever missing.
     - AssignedTo has 5 names, plus 3 unassigned rows.
     - Most rows have descriptions, and a few leave it null so that case is
       exercised.

     TaskItemConfiguration.Configure gets one line,
     builder.HasData(SeedData.Tasks). RowVersion is
     store-generated, so EF leaves it out of InsertData; I'll confirm that in
     the generated migration.

     Migration

     dotnet ef migrations add SeedTasks --project src/TaskManager.Api 
     --output-dir Data/Migrations.
     It's expected to contain only InsertData (Up) and DeleteData (Down) for ids
     1–15. EF wraps the insert in
     SET IDENTITY_INSERT, and SQL Server moves the identity past 15, so new
     tasks start at 16.
     I'll check with dotnet ef migrations script InitialCreate SeedTasks before
     running tests.
     Reminder: this data goes into every DB the migration reaches. That's the
     trade-off you accepted for HasData.

     How the seed is tested despite Respawn

     Respawn empties Tasks before every DB test, so no test can rely on seed
     rows surviving a reset.
     I'd test the seed in two layers:

     1. Unit test of the data itself (Unit/SeedDataTests.cs, no DB). It runs on
        SeedData.Tasks and checks:
        - there are 15 rows with unique ids 1–15
        - the active rows contain each of the 12 status × priority pairs exactly
          once
        - exactly 3 rows are deleted
        - there are at least 4 distinct AssignedTo values and at least one null
        - every date is UTC, ModifiedDate ≥ CreatedDate, and CreatedDate values
          are distinct
        - Title, Description and AssignedTo fit the column max lengths, using
          the TaskItemConfiguration constants

        These tests are fast and say exactly which rule broke.
     2. Integration test that the migration really inserts it
        (Integration/Database/SeedMigrationTests.cs, in the
        Database collection). It re-runs the seed migration on the shared test
        DB:
        - IMigrator.MigrateAsync("InitialCreate") runs SeedTasks.Down, which is
          a harmless DeleteData
          because Respawn already emptied the table.
        - IMigrator.MigrateAsync() runs SeedTasks.Up again and inserts the 15
          rows.
        - Assert, with IgnoreQueryFilters, 15 rows whose ids, titles, status,
          priority, IsDeleted and AssignedTo
          equal SeedData.Tasks. Without the filter there should be 12 visible.
        - Assert a newly added task gets an Id > 15, which proves the identity
          moved past the seed.
        - Respawn wipes the table again before the next test, so other tests
          stay isolated. If the test dies
          mid-way, the fixture's MigrateAsync() on the next run puts the DB back
          on the latest migration.

        This needs no extra database. It exercises the real migration SQL, not
        just the model, and it stays inside
        the _Tests guard.

     Files

     - New:
       - src/TaskManager.Api/Data/SeedData.cs
       - Data/Migrations/<ts>_SeedTasks.cs and .Designer.cs
       - tests/TaskManager.Tests/Unit/SeedDataTests.cs
       - tests/TaskManager.Tests/Integration/Database/SeedMigrationTests.cs
     - Edited:
       - Data/Configurations/TaskItemConfiguration.cs (the HasData line)
       - Data/Migrations/TaskDbContextModelSnapshot.cs (regenerated)
       - README.md (a seed data section)

     Verification

     dotnet build
     dotnet test
     dotnet ef database update --project src/TaskManager.Api        # applies 
     SeedTasks to TaskManagerDb
     Then, in SSMS, against TaskManagerDb:
     SELECT Id, Title, Status, Priority, AssignedTo, IsDeleted, CreatedDate FROM
     dbo.Tasks ORDER BY Id;  -- 15 rows
     SELECT IsDeleted, COUNT(*) FROM dbo.Tasks GROUP BY IsDeleted;
                             -- 0:12, 1:3
     SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) FROM dbo.Tasks WHERE IsDeleted = 0 GROUP
     BY Status, Priority;      -- 12 rows, each 1
     SELECT MigrationId FROM dbo.__EFMigrationsHistory;
                              -- InitialCreate, SeedTasks

● Step 3 is done and dotnet test passes all 48 tests (10 new). I haven't
  committed anything, started step 4, or run dotnet ef database update on your
  dev database; that's the first verification step below.

  The migration SQL

  This is from dotnet ef migrations script InitialCreate SeedTasks, shortened to
  the first and last rows:
  BEGIN TRANSACTION;
  IF EXISTS (SELECT * FROM [sys].[identity_columns] WHERE [name] IN (N'Id',
  N'AssignedTo', N'CreatedDate',
  N'Description', N'IsDeleted', N'ModifiedDate', N'Priority', N'Status',
  N'Title') AND [object_id] = OBJECT_ID(N'[Tasks]'))
      SET IDENTITY_INSERT [Tasks] ON;
  INSERT INTO [Tasks] ([Id], [AssignedTo], [CreatedDate], [Description],
  [IsDeleted], [ModifiedDate], [Priority], [Status], [Title])
  VALUES (1, N'Bob', '2026-01-02T09:00:00.0000000+00:00', N'Move 2025 project 
  folders to cold storage.', CAST(0 AS bit),
  '2026-01-02T09:00:00.0000000+00:00', N'Low', N'Todo', N'Archive old project 
  files'),
  ...
  (15, NULL, '2026-01-16T12:00:00.0000000+00:00', NULL, CAST(1 AS bit),
  '2026-01-16T12:00:00.0000000+00:00', N'Low',
  N'Done', N'Old test task');
  IF EXISTS (...) SET IDENTITY_INSERT [Tasks] OFF;
  INSERT INTO [__EFMigrationsHistory] ([MigrationId], [ProductVersion]) VALUES
  (N'20261120125105_SeedTasks', N'9.0.10');
  COMMIT;
  RowVersion isn't in the INSERT column list, so SQL Server generates it. Every
  date is a fixed UTC value. Run the same command to see all 15 rows.

  How the seed is tested despite Respawn

  - Unit tests (SeedDataTests, no database):
    - ids are exactly 1–15
    - the 12 active rows cover all 12 status × priority pairs once
    - exactly 3 rows are deleted
    - there are at least 4 assignees plus unassigned rows
    - dates are UTC, ModifiedDate is never before CreatedDate, and CreatedDate
      values are distinct
    - text fits the column limits
    - each read returns fresh copies, so a test can't accidentally change the
      seed
  - Integration tests (SeedMigrationTests): each test re-runs the seed migration
    itself, rolling back to InitialCreate and forward again. It then checks:
    - all 15 rows match SeedData field by field, and each has a RowVersion
    - only 12 rows are visible through the soft-delete filter
    - a new task gets an Id above 15

    Each test wipes the table again afterwards. A partly failed run is repaired
    by the fixture's migrate step on the next run.

  How to verify

  dotnet build
  dotnet test
  dotnet ef database update --project src/TaskManager.Api    # applies SeedTasks
  to TaskManagerDb

  SSMS queries for TaskManagerDb

  I ran these against TaskManagerDb_Tests and they execute cleanly. That
  database only holds leftover rows from the context tests, so the counts below
  are what you should see in TaskManagerDb.
  USE TaskManagerDb;

  -- 1. All 15 seed rows
  SELECT Id, Title, Status, Priority, AssignedTo, IsDeleted, CreatedDate,
  ModifiedDate
  FROM dbo.Tasks ORDER BY Id;

  -- 2. Active vs deleted: expect 0 → 12, 1 → 3
  SELECT IsDeleted, COUNT(*) AS Tasks FROM dbo.Tasks GROUP BY IsDeleted;

  -- 3. Coverage: expect 12 rows, each with Tasks = 1
  SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) AS Tasks
  FROM dbo.Tasks WHERE IsDeleted = 0
  GROUP BY Status, Priority ORDER BY Status, Priority;

  -- 4. Assignees: Alice 3, Bob 2, Chen 3, Dana 2, Eve 2, (unassigned) 3
  SELECT COALESCE(AssignedTo, '(unassigned)') AS AssignedTo, COUNT(*) AS Tasks
  FROM dbo.Tasks GROUP BY AssignedTo;

  -- 5. Identity has moved past the seed: expect 15 (the next insert gets 16)
  SELECT IDENT_CURRENT('dbo.Tasks') AS CurrentIdentity;

  -- 6. Migrations: InitialCreate and SeedTasks
  SELECT MigrationId, ProductVersion FROM dbo.__EFMigrationsHistory ORDER BY
  MigrationId;

  Things you should know

  - I named the helper inside SeedData.cs Item(...) rather than Task(...), so it
    doesn't shadow .NET's Task type.
  - On the first try, the migration script command said "migration not found".
    That was only because I ran it with --no-build straight after adding the
    migration. Running it again with a build worked.
  - Promp.txt is still untracked in the repo root.

● Approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: 
     ~\.claude\plans\pasted-content-id-0656-i-m-working-expressive-umbrella.md ·
     /plan to edit
     Step 4: Create + Get by id

     Context

     Steps 1–3 are committed (latest 9ccea93): ProblemDetails/exception handler,
     CORS, TaskItem + TaskDbContext
     with the soft-delete filter, a Respawn test fixture, and 15 seed tasks.
     Step 4 adds the first real endpoints,
     POST /api/tasks and GET /api/tasks/{id}. They go through DTOs and a
     TaskService behind an interface.
     Every error must come back in the same ProblemDetails /
     ValidationProblemDetails shape.

     Decisions (all four confirmed by you)

     1. Bad input → 400 ValidationProblemDetails, keyed by field name.
        - Status and Priority are string in the request DTO, validated by a
          custom [EnumName(typeof(TaskPriority))]
          attribute. The error looks like errors.Priority: ["Priority must be 
          one of: Low, Medium, High, Critical."].
        - The comparison ignores case; the service parses the value. Numeric
          enum values are rejected.
        - Why not typed enums in the request: with typed enums, System.Text.Json
          fails during deserialization. The
          error key then becomes $.priority, the message leaks
          TaskManager.Api.Domain.TaskPriority, and
          JsonStringEnumConverter accepts integers by default, so "priority": 99
          would get through.
        - Title: [Required] (rejects null, "" and whitespace) and
          [StringLength(TaskItemConfiguration.TitleMaxLength)].
          Description and AssignedTo use [StringLength] with their constants.
        - Malformed JSON: AllowInputFormatterExceptionMessages = false gives a
          generic "The input was not valid."
          under a $... key, with no parser or type details.
          SuppressImplicitRequiredAttributeForNonNullableReferenceTypes = true
          stops the noisy extra "request field is required" error; every
          required field gets an explicit [Required].
        - Response enums still serialize as strings. I'll also set
          JsonStringEnumConverter(allowIntegerValues: false).
     2. Create → 201 Created + Location: /api/tasks/{id} + the full TaskResponse
        body, the same shape GET returns.
        The UI gets server values (id, dates, rowVersion) without a second call.
        This uses CreatedAtAction(nameof(GetById), …).
     3. rowVersion is in TaskResponse as a base64 string (System.Text.Json's
        default for byte[]). Step 6's PUT
        sends it back. The ETag/If-Match alternative would be more HTTP-correct,
        but it needs CORS
        WithExposedHeaders("ETag") and header plumbing in the UI. A body field
        is simpler.
     4. Soft-deleted id → 404, identical to an unknown id, so a deleted task's
        existence isn't revealed. The global
        filter hides the row, the service throws NotFoundException, and the
        existing handler returns 404 ProblemDetails.
        id = 0 (or "abc") → 404, because the route constraint {id:int:min(1)}
        doesn't match. It goes through status code pages, so it's still
        ProblemDetails.

     Smaller defaults (say if you disagree):
     - Status is optional on create and defaults to Todo. Priority is required.
     - Title, Description and AssignedTo are trimmed, and a blank Description or
       AssignedTo becomes null.
     - Unknown JSON fields (id, isDeleted, createdDate…) are ignored. The DTO
       doesn't have them, so clients can't over-post.

     Code

     - Contracts/CreateTaskRequest.cs: a class with { get; init; } properties
       plus DataAnnotations, using the
       TaskItemConfiguration length constants.
     - Contracts/TaskResponse.cs: a record with Id, Title, Description, Status,
       Priority, AssignedTo, CreatedDate,
       ModifiedDate and RowVersion. There's no IsDeleted, because visible tasks
       are never deleted.
     - Contracts/TaskMappings.cs: TaskItem.ToResponse().
     - Validation/EnumNameAttribute.cs: a ValidationAttribute that accepts null
       ([Required] handles that) and
       checks Enum.TryParse(ignoreCase) plus Enum.IsDefined (which rejects "1"
       and similar). The message lists the allowed names.
     - Services/ITaskService.cs / TaskService.cs:
       - It depends on TaskDbContext, TimeProvider and ILogger<TaskService>.
       - CreateAsync(CreateTaskRequest, CancellationToken): now = 
         timeProvider.GetUtcNow(), CreatedDate = ModifiedDate = now,
         trim, save, then log Created task {TaskId} ({Status}, {Priority}).
       - GetByIdAsync(int id, CancellationToken): AsNoTracking, or throw
         NotFoundException("Task", id).
     - Controllers/TasksController.cs: [ApiController] [Route("api/tasks")]. Two
       actions, each a single call to the service,
       with XML docs and [ProducesResponseType] for 201/400 and 200/404.
     - Program.cs / ServiceCollectionExtensions:
       AddSingleton(TimeProvider.System), AddScoped<ITaskService, 
       TaskService>(), and the
       JSON/MVC options from decision 1.

     Tests

     Packages (Directory.Packages.props):
     - Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.InMemory 9.0.10
     - Microsoft.Extensions.TimeProvider.Testing 9.0.0. This package uses its
       own 9.x minor line (the latest is 9.10.0).
       I'm pinning 9.0.0 to follow your "9.0.x only" rule.

     Unit (Unit/TaskServiceTests.cs, EF InMemory + FakeTimeProvider):
     - CreateAsync_SetsCreatedAndModifiedDate_ToCurrentUtcTime: with a fake now
       of 2026-03-01T10:00Z,
       both dates equal it, and they equal each other.
     - CreateAsync_TrimsText_AndDefaultsStatusToTodo
     - GetByIdAsync_SoftDeletedTask_ThrowsNotFound

     Each test gets a fresh InMemory DB with a unique name. RowVersion isn't
     generated by InMemory, and these tests don't need it.

     Integration (Integration/Tasks/CreateTaskTests.cs, GetTaskTests.cs, 
     Database collection, via fixture.Factory):
     - Create, success cases:
       - 201, Location = /api/tasks/{id}, and the body has a non-empty
         rowVersion and CreatedDate == ModifiedDate
       - GET on the Location returns the same body
       - a title of exactly 200 characters → 201
       - Status omitted → Todo
       - lowercase "high" → High
       - over-posted id/isDeleted are ignored
     - Create, failures (each is 400 application/problem+json with a traceId and
       an error under the named key):
       - missing title, blank "   " title, 201-character title → Title
       - Description 2001 characters, AssignedTo 101 characters
       - "Blocked" → Status, "Urgent" → Priority
       - Priority missing
       - numeric "priority": 2
       - malformed JSON {"title":, which must not contain "System.Text.Json",
         "Exception" or "TaskManager.Api"
       - empty body
       - text/plain body → 415 ProblemDetails
     - Get:
       - existing → 200 with every field
       - unknown 999999 → 404 with detail "Task with id '999999' was not found."
       - soft-deleted → 404 with the same title/detail pattern as unknown
       - /api/tasks/0 → 404
       - /api/tasks/abc → 404

     All tests pass under dotnet test.

     Files

     - New:
       - API: Contracts/{CreateTaskRequest,TaskResponse,TaskMappings}.cs,
         Validation/EnumNameAttribute.cs,
         Services/{ITaskService,TaskService}.cs, Controllers/TasksController.cs
       - Tests: Unit/TaskServiceTests.cs, Unit/EnumNameAttributeTests.cs,
         Integration/Tasks/{CreateTaskTests,GetTaskTests}.cs
     - Edited: Program.cs, Infrastructure/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs,
       Directory.Packages.props,
       TaskManager.Tests.csproj, TaskManager.Api.http, README.md

     - POST a valid task → 201 with a Location header
     - GET that Location → 200
     - GET /api/tasks/13 (a seeded, soft-deleted task) → 404
     - GET /api/tasks/0 → 404
     - POST {"title":"","priority":"Urgent"} → 400 with errors.Title and
       errors.Priority

  src/TaskManager.Api/Validation/EnumNameAttribute.cs

  using System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations;

  namespace TaskManager.Api.Validation;

  /// <summary>
  /// Validates that a string is the name of a member of <see cref="EnumType"/> 
  (case-insensitive).
  /// Numbers, comma-separated flag combinations and unknown names are rejected.
  Null is treated as
  /// valid; combine with <see cref="RequiredAttribute"/> when a value is 
  mandatory.
  /// </summary>
  [AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Property | AttributeTargets.Parameter | 
  AttributeTargets.Field)]
  public sealed class EnumNameAttribute : ValidationAttribute
  {
      public EnumNameAttribute(Type enumType)
      {
          ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(enumType);
          if (!enumType.IsEnum)
          {
              throw new ArgumentException($"{enumType.Name} is not an enum
  type.", nameof(enumType));
          }

          EnumType = enumType;
      }

      public Type EnumType { get; }

      public override bool IsValid(object? value) =>
          value is null || (value is string text && FindName(EnumType, text) is
  not null);

      public override string FormatErrorMessage(string name) =>
          $"{name} must be one of: {string.Join(", ", 
  Enum.GetNames(EnumType))}.";

      /// Parses a value that has already passed validation. Throws if the value
  is not a member name.
      public static TEnum Parse<TEnum>(string value)
          where TEnum : struct, Enum =>
          FindName(typeof(TEnum), value) is { } name
              ? Enum.Parse<TEnum>(name)
              : throw new ArgumentException($"'{value}' is not a valid
  {typeof(TEnum).Name}.", nameof(value));

      private static string? FindName(Type enumType, string value) =>
          Enum.GetNames(enumType).FirstOrDefault(name => string.Equals(name,
  value, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase));
  }
  (The real file also has XML doc comments; I've left them out here.)

  Why it compares against the enum's names instead of calling Enum.TryParse:
  Enum.TryParse accepts "2", and it also accepts "Low, High". That second one
  combines into the value 2, which is High, so both strings would get through.
  Comparing against the declared names only lets real names in.

  It doesn't trim either, so " Low" is rejected.

  src/TaskManager.Api/Services/TaskService.cs

  using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
  using TaskManager.Api.Contracts;
  using TaskManager.Api.Data;
  using TaskManager.Api.Domain;
  using TaskManager.Api.Validation;

  namespace TaskManager.Api.Services;

  /// <summary>EF Core implementation of <see cref="ITaskService"/>.</summary>
  public sealed class TaskService : ITaskService
  {
      private const string ResourceName = "Task";

      private readonly TaskDbContext _db;
      private readonly TimeProvider _timeProvider;
      private readonly ILogger<TaskService> _logger;

      public TaskService(TaskDbContext db, TimeProvider timeProvider,
  ILogger<TaskService> logger)
      {
          _db = db;
          _timeProvider = timeProvider;
          _logger = logger;
      }

      public async Task<TaskResponse> CreateAsync(CreateTaskRequest request,
  CancellationToken cancellationToken)
      {
          ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(request);

          var now = _timeProvider.GetUtcNow();
          var task = new TaskItem
          {
              Title = request.Title!.Trim(),
              Description = NullIfBlank(request.Description),
              Status = request.Status is null ? TaskItemStatus.Todo :
  EnumNameAttribute.Parse<TaskItemStatus>(request.Status),
              Priority =
  EnumNameAttribute.Parse<TaskPriority>(request.Priority!),
              AssignedTo = NullIfBlank(request.AssignedTo),
              CreatedDate = now,
              ModifiedDate = now,
          };

          _db.Tasks.Add(task);
          await _db.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken);

          _logger.LogInformation("Created task {TaskId} with status {Status} and
  priority {Priority}", task.Id, task.Status, task.Priority);
          return task.ToResponse();
      }

      public async Task<TaskResponse> GetByIdAsync(int id, CancellationToken 
  cancellationToken)
      {
          var task = await _db.Tasks.AsNoTracking().FirstOrDefaultAsync(t =>
  t.Id == id, cancellationToken)
              ?? throw new NotFoundException(ResourceName, id);

          return task.ToResponse();
      }

      private static string? NullIfBlank(string? value) =>
          string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(value) ? null : value.Trim();
  }
  Things to look at:
  - Time is read once. GetUtcNow() is called once, so CreatedDate and
    ModifiedDate always come out identical.
  - The ! operators on Title and Priority are safe because [ApiController]
    rejects the request before the service runs if either is missing.
  - Soft-deleted ids: GetByIdAsync relies on the global query filter. A
    soft-deleted row is never found, so it gets the same NotFoundException as an
    unknown id.
  - What isn't logged: the not-found case isn't logged here, because
    GlobalExceptionHandler already logs it at Warning with the traceId. Request
    bodies are never logged.

  Other changes so far:
  - CreateTaskRequest, TaskResponse, TaskMappings and TasksController are
    written.
  - ServiceCollectionExtensions has AddApiControllers() (the JSON options) and
    AddTaskServices() (registers TimeProvider.System and ITaskService).
  - The tests are written but not run: TaskServiceTests with FakeTimeProvider,
    EnumNameAttributeTests, CreateTaskTests and GetTaskTests.

  Tell me to go ahead, along with any changes to these two files, and I'll run
  dotnet test, update the README and .http, and give you the requests to try by
  hand.

  Requests to try by hand

  Start the API with dotnet run --project src/TaskManager.Api --launch-profile

  intact):
  # Create: 201, Location: /api/tasks/{id}, full task body including rowVersion
  curl.exe -i -X POST http://localhost:5157/api/tasks -H "Content-Type: 
  application/json" -d '{\"title\":\"Try the API by 
  hand\",\"priority\":\"High\",\"assignedTo\":\"Alice\"}'

  # Get the task you just created (use the id from the Location header): 200, 
  same body
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/api/tasks/16

  # Status omitted, lowercase priority: 201 with "status":"Todo", 
  "priority":"Critical"
  curl.exe -i -X POST http://localhost:5157/api/tasks -H "Content-Type: 
  application/json" -d '{\"title\":\"Defaults\",\"priority\":\"critical\"}'

  # Blank title + unknown priority: 400, errors.Title and errors.Priority
  curl.exe -i -X POST http://localhost:5157/api/tasks -H "Content-Type: 
  application/json" -d '{\"title\":\"   \",\"priority\":\"Urgent\"}'

  # Numeric priority: 400, errors["$.priority"] = "The input was not valid."
  curl.exe -i -X POST http://localhost:5157/api/tasks -H "Content-Type: 
  application/json" -d '{\"title\":\"x\",\"priority\":2}'

  # Malformed JSON: 400, no parser or type details
  curl.exe -i -X POST http://localhost:5157/api/tasks -H "Content-Type: 
  application/json" -d '{\"title\":'

  # Seeded active task: 200
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/api/tasks/1
  # Seeded soft-deleted task: 404, same shape as an unknown id
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/api/tasks/13
  # Unknown id: 404 "Task with id '999999' was not found."
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/api/tasks/999999
  # id = 0: 404 problem+json
  curl.exe -i http://localhost:5157/api/tasks/0

  Here is what came back when I ran these:
  {"title":"One or more validation errors 
  occurred.","status":400,"instance":"POST /api/tasks",
   "errors":{"Title":["Title is required."],"Priority":["Priority must be one 
  of: Low, Medium, High, Critical."]},"traceId":"00-…"}
  {"title":"Resource not found","status":404,"detail":"Task with id '999999' was
  not found.","instance":"GET /api/tasks/999999","traceId":"00-…"}

  Things you should know

  - Malformed JSON key: the error is reported under the JSON path where parsing
    stopped, e.g. $.title for {"title":. It isn't a plain $ key. The message is
    always the generic "The input was not valid."
  - Error key casing: validation keys use C# property names (Title, Priority),
    while the JSON body uses camelCase (title). The UI's error mapping in step
    10 will need to match case-insensitively. I can switch the keys to camelCase
    if you'd rather.
  - Test database rows: my manual run created a couple of tasks in
    TaskManagerDb_Tests. Respawn clears them on the next test run.
  - Promp.txt: git now shows it as modified, but I didn't touch it.
